# Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution

A release is immutable source over one durable store. JSON fields, bounded ready waits and worker roots: `guide release-json`. Deploy from the app directory:

```sh
bunx snapback4 check
bunx snapback4 test
bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
```
For a complete real-user staging script and first-admin setup, run `guide staging`. Bulk account setup is restricted to fresh `deploy --staging` stores. Generations climb on every actual switch, including rollback and forward; selecting identical execution content is a no-op. `token --service ops --release ./deployment` works while serve runs. Signups are rate-limited: create proof accounts sequentially and respect a returned retry delay. Time-gated mutations on the real clock need a bounded retry of the authored refusal (for example `NOT_OPEN`), with a deadline and short delay; never retry unrelated refusals. Development proofs can advance the clock (`guide time`).

## Upgrade
`snapback4 upgrade [version]` resolves once and exactly pins core and installed Expo adapters with npm/bun/pnpm. Rebuild native apps after adapter changes; commit the lockfile, run check/typecheck/acceptance. `test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. Use the password and first-admin recipes below.

## Deploy and password identity
Add `use identity password` (included below). Deploy from the app directory onto durable storage:
Serve admits bearer sessions; personas are 401 and /seed/dev doors 403, even through proxies. Signup creates an account, not a privileged role. See guide client for SDK session stores; auth/headless for account setup. HTTP auth returns session/denied; SDK signup/login from `snapback4/auth` returns ok/why. Check warns on installed version skew.
```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
# {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
```
Keep the session and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Wrong credentials or a disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. Sessions survive ordinary deployments and restarts until expiry/revocation; a replaced/reset store requires sign-in again. Never delete a store to make adoption pass. `serve --port N` accepts 0 or A-B; stdout's first line is its URL, stderr includes SNAPBACK4_READY. Wait for readiness.

Layout: releases/N/ holds source; store.sqlite data/sessions/queues; assets/ uploads. The database owns current release/history; current is a text convenience copy, releases.jsonl a convenience ledger. Back up the whole directory, including sidecars/assets/releases, with serve and external workers stopped (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6). A restore does not rewind external effects.

## First admin: a service seat
Provision roles through an operator seat, with no public first-user race:
```quarry
use identity password
table admins:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  sync
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
  return insert admins { principal }
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false
  status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
```
```sh
TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
curl -s "$U/m/grantAdmin" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"principal":"user:SIGNED_UP_ID"}}'
unset TOKEN
```
This roster is synced. A whole service-seat branch may add the first member; ordinary callers must already hold the roster. Service grants add no device audience. The seat's own program lookups and `exists` obey read rules too: to inspect a roster, add `or viewer = 'service:ops'` to its read rule, as above. A rule probe has authorization visibility; a program probe does not. The mutation completes before the grant syncs; wait for the enrolled account's client to receive its scope before using offline staff reads.

A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`). Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.

## Migrations and adoption
Optional/defaulted columns, new tables/ops, non-unique indexes and wider types are additive. Required new fields need a literal default or explicit backfill; default now/viewer cannot invent historical values. Dev check/adoption refuses incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Release type narrowing instead retains each row whose present values cannot fit. Unique/cap conflicts still refuse atomically; repair active rows, or restore through a widened non-unique bridge, repair, then reinstate constraints. Prefer expand → migrate → contract for client compatibility.

## Rollback after widening, restore and prune
```sh
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1
```
`deploy ./deployment --check` previews deploy direction and restoration intent. `bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1` also previews a selection; `bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest` inspects it. Forward with `bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest`. Preview runs exact adoption on a private disk snapshot and removes it, without activating; disk/time scales with the store, and later writes can change the plan. Release is immutable source; latest is the highest successfully adopted source even after rollback. A refused deploy removes its candidate directory and private stage. An ambiguous reply preserves source until committed history resolves the outcome; inspect releases before retrying. Bare rollback selects the previously active release; rollback prints the resolved target before preview or activation. Generation is a monotone identity; releases N/latest inspects source. Source identical to the active release is a no-op; source matching a retained release reactivates its existing id with restoration and full validation. Deploy/rollback return after serve switches: `release N is current as generation G`. In-flight one-shot operations finish in their generation; no settle sleep is needed. Default adoption is one measured receipt; --verbose/--json includes phases/cache advice. N_ADOPTION_PAUSE appears above one second. A dev/older unreachable owner requires stopping that owned process before retrying; failed adoption keeps current.

Widened enum values, or short codes a loose release wrote, become dormant rows when selected types cannot hold them; compatible forward restores exact values. Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Point reads/updates/ deletes see absent rows; dormant ids stay reserved. Nominal reference values stay legal, as for other missing ids; require/read-through decides live-target admission. Dormant rows occupy no cap/unique slots, so older writes can require a bridge repair. Removed tables/columns/indexes also stay dormant. Fresh-source name reuse needs --restore-dormant; selecting retained source by deploy or rollback already selects restoration. The same --restore-dormant flag is accepted by both commands. Old active updates keep dormant cells; active deletion removes them. Only active referential actions run: parent deletion invalidates dormant lifetimes. Narrowed rows with invalid visible references stay dormant; whole-table/column restoration retains its E_ADOPT barrier. Prune previews counts/bytes/sample ids; `prune ./deployment --allow-destructive` permanently drops all dormant storage. No age collector or implicit pruning exists. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive bypasses neither validation nor retention. --allow-feed-adoption permits the explicit feed launch. Inspect releases after an ambiguous reply. Details: [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

Check business compatibility separately: old payment code may accept payment while a new review row is dormant. Rollback preview identifies current safety guards/rules and read confidentiality the target loses, active table-population changes, and current → target quotas. Preserve the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release, or pause that workflow. Old source cannot supply absent product logic. An absent operation returns 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP",…},generation:G}`; a mutation's incompatible arguments return 200 `{state:"failed",why:{code:"E_INPUT",…},generation:G}`.

## Bridge a safety gate before rollback
Use this beside the enum-widening rollback recipe above. Suppose v2 added `grounding.state = 'inspection'`; v1 holds only `'clear'`/`'hold'`. Selecting v1 hides that row and its children, so v1's `book` guard may allow a booking that v2 blocks. Counts and sample ids are snapshot dependencies, not predicate matches; later writes can change the plan.

1. Start from the old UI/operations, but keep v2's widened `grounding.state` domain, safety table, read audience and booking `require`. Save this complete source as `versions/bridge/schema.q` and deploy it with `deploy ./deployment --schema versions/bridge/schema.q --check`, then without `--check`.
2. While the gate still runs, migrate each inspection to a truthful old-domain hold through an authorized bridge mutation. Do not relabel it clear just to satisfy v1. Keep linked metadata until its parent can be safely represented; if v1 cannot invalidate newer metadata, keep that behavior in the bridge too.
3. Prove the same booking/payment still refuses for the blocked case and that the named lists/counts preserve the intended business denominator. Re-run the final old-source preview against the repaired live population.
4. Select v1 only when those checks pass. If the old source cannot preserve the invariant, keep the bridge or pause that workflow. Retained storage cannot supply absent logic.

## What the old release sees
| Request after rollback | Result |
|---|---|
| An operation absent from the old release | HTTP 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP"}}` |
| Bare point query of a dormant row | Reads null |
| Point query or mutation with an explicit `! else CODE` guard | Refuses its guard code (`NOT_FOUND` when authored that way) |
| Added columns after rollback | Ignored by the older release; their cells stay dormant |
| Counts, rules, maintained views and sync | Only the active population |
| Compatible forward to the newer release | Original dormant values restored |

This proof assumes v1 declares `readItem(item)` returning `items[item]` and `editItem(item)` with `row = items[item]! else NOT_FOUND`; v2 adds `newOnly` and writes an enum value v1 cannot hold. Keep the bearer session in `headers`:
```js
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', '1'); const absent = await fetch(url + '/q/newOnly', {method:'POST', headers, body:'{"args":{}}'});
assert.equal(absent.status, 404); assert.equal((await absent.json()).denied.code, 'E_OP');
assert.equal((await post('/m/editItem', {item})).why.code, 'NOT_FOUND');
assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data, null);
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', 'latest'); assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data.status, 'new');
```
Previews lead with removed guards and their refusal codes, then group query/count active-table losses, linked dormant rows and safety dependencies per change. Verify filtered/paged/per-viewer results and denominators. Literal-default backfills print `table.column ← value on N rows` in previews and cold/hot deploy receipts: interpreted historical fields, not physical writes. Preview shows old and new rule clauses and mutation `require`s side by side, preserving Boolean parentheses. A proved tightening is a note, including `require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` when A's refusal contract is unchanged; changed codes/messages still warn. A loosening or unproved implication is a WARNING, including rollback that removes a new guard. Unchanged clauses and consulted keys, fields, rules, auth and visibility are silent. Unread column domains and forward domain widening do not unprove a probe; narrowing still gets exact dormant-value/population warnings. Changed dependencies group once: verify refusal cases. Comparison never changes admission. Missing operations/schedules, populated removed tables, changed clauses and job bodies are named separately. An unchanged job compiling differently after a dependency change gets a note. Original read-through captures restore only with the same capture definition; new uncaptured rows still need safe audience correspondence. Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.

## Clients across rollback
Save versions/v1/schema.q (and any other .q modules with their original names) outside snapback/ and create `snapback/client-compatibility.json` containing `{"baseline":"versions/v1","since_release":2}`. `check`/`dev` emit added row/result fields as optional, labelled "Since release 2", retaining older result value domains. Required operation parameters remain required; only declared optional, defaulted or deprecated arguments may be omitted. Baseline generation also warns about old-client input breaks and result/refusal changes. This is your release label, not server generation; ordinary types stay required without opt-in. Use `row.color ?? "none"` for the baseline fallback. Keep contemporary fixtures in versions/v1/fixtures/; deploy with `--schema versions/v1` and test with `--source versions/v1` (guide testing). Baselines resolve from the original project root, including saved-source deploys.

`useRelease()` from snapback4/react follows the authenticated catalog even when query gates are closed: loading, denied, or `{generation, operations, tables}` with public argument/field shapes. Generated `api.op.available(release)` is false while unready or when name/kind/existing-argument signatures differ or the server adds a required argument; extra optional served parameters are compatible. This checks capability, not authorization or unchanged business semantics; still handle E_OP/E_INPUT and authored guard refusals when a switch races it. The SDK withdraws old metadata on observed generations, polls while subscribed, and returns loading offline/on catalog failure; account retirement/close withdraws it. Keep draft state above generation-sensitive read/action gates; remount only the gated children. Dormant means unavailable on this release, not deleted. Test v2 → v1 → v2 with one frontend: field fallbacks, missing operations, retained drafts and restored values. Alternatively check selected immutable source in a separate copy and rebuild its matching frontend. Preserve deployment source; Expo/Exact runtime changes also require a device rebuild.

## Trigger a deployed job
A running serve accepts `POST /jobs/<job>` with a full service token; the job executes as the system on its FIFO queue and server clock. Every full service seat can trigger system jobs, so provision these tokens only to trusted operators. Guest/account sessions, read-scoped tokens and personas cannot use this door. `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` remains development-only.
```sh
export SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
bunx snapback4 run reconcile --release ./deployment --json
unset SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN
```
`--token-env NAME` reads another environment variable. The command uses served source, so it also works outside the app directory. A lost reply is ambiguous; inspect the queue before retrying. Starter/client recipes: `guide client`.

## Persona transition checklist
The complete account directory, linked membership and real-account transition is `guide production`.
1. Add password identity and signup/login UI; persist sessions with localStorageSessionStore on web or the secure native store, and verify restart.
2. Replace hard-coded persona owners/UI with signed-in account principals; map roles through the trusted seat recipe.
3. Give profiles a production write path and authorized directory; expose no credentials.
4. Create required teams/memberships, invitations or principal sharing; wait for scope delivery before offline reads; test expiry, revocation and outsiders.
5. Bootstrap every required table without fixtures/seed; guide start/client covers first writers and password starters.
6. Prove real-account writes, role checks, sign-out and rollback on durable storage behind TLS.

## Inspection, proxies and TLS
| Door | `dev` | `serve` |
|---|---|---|
| `/q`, `/m`, `GET /` compatibility record | Loopback persona or bearer | Bearer session required, including public rows |
| `/auth/config`; signup/login/guest | Public config; declared sign-in methods | Public config; declared sign-in methods |
| `/seed`, `/dev/*` | Development only | Refused |
| `/jobs/<job>` | Service seat | Full service seat |

Public visitors obtain guest sessions with `use identity`; `use identity password` disables guests (`guide rule-recipes`).

`snapback4 sql 'SELECT id, archived FROM notes' --release ./deployment --json` returns positional rows under `columns`; `--json-objects` returns object rows with direct boolean columns as true/false. Schema-qualified relations, recursive or shadowing/forward-reference CTEs need positional --json. Inspection bypasses read rules and can show expired stored rows; it is an operator tool, not an app query surface. Bind loopback behind a TLS proxy. Forward Authorization and preserve response bodies; allow long polls without buffering or early timeouts. Non-loopback HTTP requires `--i-understand-cleartext` and a trusted private hop behind TLS. Identity rate keys use the socket peer, not forwarded headers. Serve refuses personas and dev doors even through a local proxy; see SERVER.md for capacity. `--json-objects` returns `{header, columns, rows, limit, more, ruleFiltered:false}` with object `rows`; `--json` uses positional `rows`. Put flags outside the quoted SQL.

## Authoring and proof refusals
N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER means no production insert path can bootstrap this table. It groups root blockers with their dependents; persona-only schemas get one identity note. Add a service-seat insert mutation and provision real account relationships. E_UPGRADE means installation or post-upgrade check failed. Repair the package-manager or registry error and rerun upgrade. Packages may already be pinned: if check failed, repair the reported schema/device refusal and rerun check.

## Capture a saved version
After check and business cases pass, from the app root:
```sh
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --save-version versions/v1
snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check --bridge ./bridge
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --schema ./bridge --check
```

The new versions/v1 captures selected modules, their contemporary fixtures and `versions/v1/release.json` with the actual immutable release id, source hash and CLI version. Unchanged deploy keeps its id; existing snapshots never change. Choose a new positive-numbered versions/vN; saving cannot combine with --check. Saved-source deploy uses its own fixtures. Use the recorded id when choosing a baseline; since_release remains the author-supplied newer-schema label. The bridge starts from complete current Quarry source and this app's `bridge/ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md`. Retain current domains, audiences, guards and quotas; repair records through authorized mutations; prove blocked cases refuse and affected lists/counts remain correct; repeat the old-source preview. No bridge is activated or business migration synthesized. JSON includes checklist; --verbose retains full evidence/index paths. Dormant rows reserve ids but no unique slots: new active rows may block restoration. A widened non-unique bridge permits repair before reinstating uniqueness; retained storage cannot supply absent safety logic.

## Keep v1 clients working

`deploy --check --json` reports `clientCompatibility` separately from data
adoption: missing operations, changed kinds, required new arguments, removed or
narrowed inputs, result shape/value changes and changed refusal codes/messages.
Deploy prints the same warnings. Input breaks name the old release and the
E_INPUT/E_OP outcome. A result or behavior review asks you to verify the intended
contract; a correct new privacy denial is intentional behavior, not an input
shape failure. The comparison is conservative, not a proof of unchanged logic.

For example, save this v1 source and its callers sending
`{name:"Ada",obsolete:"old input"}` to `greet`:

```quarry
use identity guests
query greet(name: text <=40, obsolete: text <=40):
  return {message: name + '!'}
```

Deploy this v2 source. The unchanged v1 call still returns `{message:"Ada!"}`;
new callers can use `greeting` and omit `suffix` and `obsolete`:

```quarry
use identity guests
query greeting(name: text <=40, suffix: text <=1 default '!', obsolete: text <=40 deprecated):
  return {message: name + suffix}
alias greet = greeting
```

@@ -1377,3 +1377,19 @@ fn restoration_conflict_samples_keep_distinct_duplicate_keys_separate() {
     assert!(!error.contains("active rows [a1") && !error.contains("active rows [a2"),"{error}");
     assert_eq!(e.store().count("rows").unwrap(),2);

+
+#[test]
+fn compiler_intent_indexes_do_not_require_authored_restoration_but_authored_indexes_do() {
+    let indexed=V1.replace("  n: int","  by byNumber: n, id\n  n: int");
+    for (name,expected) in [("byNumber",false),("$intent:rel5",true)] {
+        let (mut s,p)=compile(&indexed);
+        let table=s.tables.get_mut("rows").unwrap();
+        let index=table.indexes.remove("byNumber").unwrap();table.indexes.insert(name.into(),index);
+        let mut e=Engine::new_release(Store::memory().unwrap(),s.clone(),p.clone(),1,None).unwrap();
+        activate(&mut e,V1,2).unwrap();
+        let options=ReleaseActivation{restore_dormant:false,..ReleaseActivation::new(3,Some(2))};
+        let result=e.adopt_release(s,p,&options,false);
+        assert_eq!(result.is_ok(),expected,"{name}: {result:?}");
+        if !expected {assert!(result.unwrap_err().to_string().contains("restoration intent"));}
+    }
+}

//! Owner-mediated release publication in the running HTTP process.
use super::*;
use crate::release_control::Command;
#[cfg(unix)]
use crate::release_control::Listener;
use snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation;

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(super) fn start(
    shared: Arc<Shared>,
    dir: std::path::PathBuf,
) -> Result<std::thread::JoinHandle<()>, String> {
    let store = shared
        .owner
        .as_ref()
        .ok_or("release control requires a persisted owner")?
        .path()
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let listener = Listener::bind(&dir, store)?;
    crate::lifecycle::managed_cleanup();
    crate::lifecycle::background("release-control", move || {
        listener.run(|command| apply(&shared, &dir, command))
    })
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — only assignments and committed rebind follow the database commit.
pub(super) fn apply(
    shared: &Shared,
    dir: &std::path::Path,
    command: Command,
) -> Result<Json, String> {
    match command {
        Command::Status {
            after,
            items_after,
            event,
        } => lock(&shared.engine)
            .store_mut()
            .release_status(after, items_after.as_deref(), event)
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string()),
        Command::Prune {
            confirm,
            expected_revision,
        } => {
            let mut result = lock(&shared.engine)
                .store_mut()
                .prune_dormant(confirm, Some(expected_revision))
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if let Some(items) = result["items"].as_array_mut() {
                items.truncate(16);
            }
            result["items_after"] = result["items"]
                .as_array()
                .and_then(|items| items.last())
                .map(|i| i["key"].clone())
                .unwrap_or(Json::Null);
            Ok(result)
        }
        Command::Activate {
            release,
            expected_release,
            expected_revision,
            digest,
            rollback,
            restore_dormant,
            allow_feed_adoption,
        } => {
            if release == 0 {
                return Err("release id must be positive".into());
            }
            let project = Project {
                root: dir.join("releases").join(release.to_string()),
            };
            if !std::fs::symlink_metadata(&project.root)
                .is_ok_and(|m| m.is_dir() && !m.file_type().is_symlink())
            {
                return Err(format!(
                    "release {release} is not an immutable release directory"
                ));
            }
            if crate::release_files::digest(&project.root)? != digest {
                return Err("staged release content changed; redeploy its exact source".into());
            }
            if project.sources().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?.is_empty() {
                return Err(format!(
                    "release {release} has no Quarry source; select a complete immutable release"
                ));
            }
            let bound = project.compile().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
                return Err(bound
                    .diagnostics
                    .iter()
                    .map(ToString::to_string)
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join("\n"));
            }
            let prepared = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project, &bound.schema)?;
            crate::release_files::link_store(&project.root)?;
            if crate::release_files::digest(&project.root)? != digest {
                return Err("release artifacts changed during preparation; retry after inspecting immutable source".into());
            }
            crate::release_files::durable(&project.root)?;
            let mut pointer =
                crate::release_files::Pointer::prepare(dir, release, expected_release, rollback)?;
            let options = ReleaseActivation {
                release,
                expected_previous: expected_release,
                expected_revision: Some(expected_revision),
                restore_dormant,
                native_digest: prepared.installed.as_ref().map(|m| m.digest.clone()),
                operation: if rollback { "rollback" } else { "deploy" }.into(),
            };
            let _requests=shared.release_requests.write();
            let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
            lock(&shared.readers).begin_adoption(&bound);
            let generation = match engine.adopt_release(
                bound.schema.clone(),
                bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()),
                &options,
                allow_feed_adoption,
            ) {
                Ok(generation) => generation,
                Err(error) => {
                    lock(&shared.readers).finish_adoption();
                    return Err(error.to_string());
                }
            };
            #[cfg(test)]
            test_work(&shared.release_committed);
            engine.set_prepared_native(prepared.module);
            engine.set_presence(Some(shared.presence.clone()));
            shared.refresh_time_audience_clock(&engine, shared.now());
            lock(&shared.readers).replace(&engine, &bound.refusal_sites);
            {
                let mut active = lock(&shared.bound);
                let mut advertised = lock(&shared.generation);
                let mut native = lock(&shared.native);
                shared.latency.adopt(&bound);
                *active = bound;
                *advertised = generation;
                *native = prepared.installed;
                let mut root = lock(&shared.release_root);
                *root = Some(project.root);
                shared.worker_revision.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Release);
            }
            crate::lifecycle::generation(generation);
            shared.publish_engine(&engine);
            // Completion I/O can fail, but never abandons the new runtime.
            pointer.finish(generation)?;
            snapback4_core::diagnostic!(
                "snapback4 serve: release {release} activated without restart as generation {generation}"
            );
            Ok(
                json!({"release":release,"generation":generation,"revision":expected_revision+1,"hot":true,"adoption":engine.store().adoption_report.compact(),"details":engine.store().adoption_report.summary(),"backfills":engine.store().adoption_report.backfills}),
            )
        }
    }
}
//! Compiled examples first; diagnostic details are under `why`.
pub const LANGUAGE: &str = r##"# Quarry — the language card

Query/mutation argument evolution: `name: T default <literal>` supplies a fitting scalar when omitted or null; `name: T deprecated` accepts and ignores the optional old input. `alias oldName = currentName` preserves a same-module endpoint. These forms keep old clients calling; the complete executable v1/v2 recipe is `guide deploy` → Keep v1 clients working. They do not change read/write authority.

**Start here:** Declare tables, rules, queries and mutations; indexes are inferred. Default-starter pattern; `guide from-sql` translates familiar idioms.

```quarry
table todos:
  title: text <=200
  ownerId: principal
  read <- .ownerId = viewer
  insert <- .ownerId = viewer
  sync to .ownerId last 100 by .id
```

- `E_QUARRY_TYPE`: wrong reference type; pass a reference to the declared table.
- `E_QUARRY_PLACEMENT`: queries cannot write; use mutations or jobs.

## Reference

```quarry
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text
  at: time default now
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  sync to .owner last 500 by .at, .id
query myNotes(c: cursor ?):
  return notes where .owner = viewer last 20 by .at after c
mutation addNote(body: text):
  return insert notes { owner: viewer, body }
```

`where .x != null` narrows loop/shape rows.

Implicit `id`; newest-first pages (`next` → `c`); atomic inserts default time.
`not before .opensAt` sets a stored lower read lifetime; null is unbounded. With `expire at`, rows live in `[opensAt, expiresAt)`. See `guide timed disclosure`.


`receipts chat by .conversation, .ordinal from .sender to members:` with field mappings generates reporting (`guide receipts`; expert recipe: `guide manual-receipts`).

## Limits

- `count(x)`, `len(x)`, `length(x)`: text characters or list items; identical typing and meters. Unsupported spellings: E_QUARRY_UNSUPPORTED.
- A field or argument must fit its declared type: E_QUARRY_TYPE.

## Details

Repeated predicates and qualified helpers:

```quarry
query familiarHelpers(kind: enum('open', 'closed')):
  return schema.kindLabel(kind)          -- a helper in schema.q also accepts its file prefix
query nestedRoomRoster(c: cursor ?):
  return members where .principal = viewer first 20 by .roomId after c { roomId, roster: members[roomId = .roomId] }
query repeatedFilters(roomId: rooms, c: cursor ?):
  return messages where .roomId = roomId where .body != null last 20 by .at after c
```

Quarry defines your tables, access rules and backend operations. Queries and eligible mutations can run on devices that hold their inputs. N_SHAPE_METER: `groupedMessages` rejects unbounded candidates; query separately.

### Tables and rules
```quarry
use identity                          -- guests and password accounts; `use identity guests` or `use identity password` admits one kind
use personas alice, bob, carol        -- a development cast: `--as alice` and the persona header act as dev:alice
effect notify                         -- a name a mutation or job may `emit` under; a worker with a service token performs it (guide effects)
table folders:
  owner: principal
  immutable owner
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  sync last 500 by .id
table documents:
  folder: folders
  body: text
  immutable folder
  read through .folder             -- the whole read rule; captures its immutable parent's audience and lifetime
  insert <- allow
  sync last 500 by .id              -- infers the captured owner audience
table rooms:
  name: text 1..80                    -- text <=N | text M..N, either with `format handle` or `format url`
  ownerId: principal                  -- principal | id | <table> (a reference to its row) | int [M..N] | time (milliseconds) | bool
  kind: enum('open', 'closed')        -- enum('a', 'b') | decimal S | money CUR [S] | json <=N | bytes <=N | image <=N [once] | video|file <=N
  tags: [text <=20] <=8               -- [T] 1..N inclusive; [T] <=N means 0..N
  parentId: rooms ? on delete set null -- `?`: optional (null if absent); every table has an `id`
  createdAt: time default now          -- omitted on insert; evaluated on the writing host
  read <- exists members[.id, viewer]                                     -- rules: allow | deny | public ['why'] | .col = viewer | .col != 'x' | exists t[..] | created t[..] | and | or | not
  insert <- .ownerId = viewer
  update (old, next) <- old.ownerId = viewer and next.ownerId = viewer   -- an update rule may name both images
  cap 50 by parentId
  after insert <- exists members[roomId = .id, principal = .ownerId, role = 'admin']                 -- a row that must exist by the end of the mutation that inserted this one
  sync to members[.id]                -- each row reaches every principal with a members row whose roomId is this row's id
table members:
  roomId: rooms on delete cascade
  principal: principal
  role: enum('member', 'admin')
  joinedAt: time
  unique roomId, principal else MEMBER_EXISTS -- one row per key
  cap 100 by roomId                                    -- maximum 100 per room
  immutable roomId, principal                          -- columns no update may change
  read <- exists members[.roomId, viewer]
  insert <- exists rooms[.roomId] and (exists members[.roomId, viewer, role = 'admin'] or created rooms[.roomId])
  delete <- .principal = viewer or exists members[.roomId, viewer, role = 'admin']
  sync to members[.roomId]
```

### Messages, uploads and expiry
```quarry
table messages:
  roomId: rooms
  ordinal: sequence by roomId         -- engine-assigned; provisional while the row is pending
  authorId: principal
  body: text <=4000 ?
  search body
  photo: image <=10000000 ?           -- an upload the row holds (guide assets); `once` after the bound serves one view per principal
  clip: video <=50000000 ?
  editedAt: time ?
  expiresAt: time ?
    bodySize = count(head.body)                                     -- guard narrows this snapshot field
  sum = sum(page, .at)
  earliest = min(page, .at)
  latest = max(page, .at)
  smallest = min([{ state: null }, { state: 1 }], .state)
  grouped = group(page, 'authorId') { author: .key, count: count(.items) }
  return { sum, earliest, latest, smallest, grouped, first: first(page), last: last(page), at: at(page, 1), total: messageCounts[roomId], counter, offsets, label, labelSize: count(label), doubled, quotient, remainder, weeks, days, lo, hi, brief: isBrief(null), oldest: sort(slice(page, 0, 10), 'at') { id, at }, smaller: min(weeks, days), larger: max(weeks, days) }
```

### Text, search and presence
```quarry
query mentions(body: text <=4000):
  return tokens(body)                 -- the @mentions in a text, without the @, at most 64: a bounded list
query tokenDigest(token: text <=200):
  return sha256(token)                -- lowercase SHA-256 of the exact UTF-8 bytes: text <=64
query search(roomId: rooms, q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  require exists members[roomId, viewer] else NOT_MEMBER
  return messages where .roomId = roomId matching q last 50 by .at after c   -- word-prefix search: uniform expansion bounded by the meter; admitted non-uniform scopes keep 32 words; newest first
query everywhere(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  return messages matching q where exists members[.roomId, viewer] last 50 by .at after c
query wholeRoster(roomId: rooms):
  return members where .roomId = roomId                         -- the schema cap is the ascending whole-group bound
query memberTotal():
  n = 0
  for m in members where .principal = viewer:                                        -- a uniform prefix under the query meter
    n = n + 1
  return n
query hasMessages(roomId: rooms):
  return exists messages where .roomId = roomId                                  -- first visible match; no page/cursor
query groupedMessages(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  return members where .principal = viewer and exists (messages[roomId = .roomId] matching q) first 20 by .roomId after c { roomId, hits: messages[roomId = .roomId] matching q last 3 by .at }
query online(p: principal):
  return connected[p]                 -- server connection count; `exists connected[p]` tests (guide presence)
```

### Mutations and writes
```quarry
mutation pagePass(roomId: rooms): server
  n = 0
  for row in members where .roomId = roomId first 10 by .principal:
    n = n + 1
  return n
mutation registerProfile(handle: text 3..20 format handle):
  return insert profiles { principal: viewer, handle }

    Require {
        condition: Expr,
        code: String,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
        site: Option<String>,
        /// The author's text for the refusal (`require … else CODE 'message'`); the code stays the contract.
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
        message: Option<String>,
    },
    /// A false predicate rolls the transaction back and re-reads `current`.
    Check { condition: Expr, current: Read },
    /// A unique clash yields absence, never the conflicting row.
    InsertIfAbsent { table: String, row: Expr, result: String },
    Insert { table: String, row: Expr },
    Update { table: String, key: Expr, patch: Expr },
    Upsert { table: String, key: Expr, row: Expr },
    /// Target lookup and the old image stay inside the engine. Only a read-filtered result is bound.
    WriteTarget { target: Read, patch: Expr, upsert: bool, condition: Option<WriteCondition>, result: Option<String> },
    Delete { table: String, key: Expr },
    /// Alternate-key delete resolves inside the engine before charging hidden expiry work.
    DeleteTarget { target: Read },
    Emit { effect: String, payload: Expr, #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] once: Option<Expr> },
    Return(Expr),
    Break,
    Continue,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct WriteCondition {
    pub old: String,
    pub setup: Vec<Stmt>,
    pub value: Expr,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum LoopDomain {
    Page(Scan),
    LiteralRange { start: i64, end_exclusive: i64 },
    ExpressionRange { start: Expr, end_exclusive: Expr },
    ArgList { name: String, max: u32 },
    DerivedArray { value: Expr, max: u32, bound_source: String },
    Recurse(Recurse),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Recurse {
    pub site: String,
    pub table: String,
    pub index: String,
    pub seed: Box<Expr>,
    pub next_field: String,
    pub depth: u32,
    pub fanout: u32,
    pub provenance: KeyProvenance,
}

pub const MAX_RECURSE_DEPTH: u32 = 10_000;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Expr {
    Literal(Value),
    Variable(String),
    Input(Input),
    Field { base: Box<Expr>, name: String },
    Array(Vec<Expr>),
    Object(BTreeMap<String, Expr>),
    Shape { base: Box<Expr>, fields: BTreeMap<String, Expr> },
    Binary { op: BinaryOp, left: Box<Expr>, right: Box<Expr> },
    Unary { op: UnaryOp, value: Box<Expr> },
    Builtin { builtin: Builtin, args: Vec<Expr> },
    Get(Read),
    Exists(Read),
    Block { body: Vec<Stmt>, value: Box<Expr> },
    Scan(Scan),
    Merge(Merge),
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Input {
    Viewer,
    Now,
    NewId,
    ReadTime,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum BinaryOp {
    Eq,
    Ne,
    SameValueZero,
    Lt,
    Lte,
    Gt,
    Gte,
    Add,
    Sub,
    Mul,
    And,
    Or,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum UnaryOp {
    Not,
    Neg,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Builtin {
    /// Server job read scope; its second argument is a read-only expression block.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    JobAs,
    /// A separately bound ordinary query, retaining its return/cursor plan.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    JobQuery { program: Box<Program>, cursor: Option<String>, all: bool },
    /// Compiler-owned projection of a successful insert result.
    ProjectFields { table: String },
    ReceiptSettled { table: String },
    /// A private field may be absent from a projected row.
    OptionalField { field: String },
    Calendar { millis: i64 },
    // @ref LLP 3000.040#22-builtins — a new tag: an older device cannot decode a program that uses it.
    Civil(crate::calendar::Civil),
    Len,
    Slice,
    Concat,
    Lower,
    Trim,
    Sha256,
    Join,
    Group { field: String, #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")] optional: bool },
    First,
    Last,
    At,
    Min,
    Max,
    /// SQL-style column extrema; domain reads require complete coverage.
    Extremum { field: String, max: bool, domain: bool },
    /// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.2 — expression extrema bind `$filter` per authorized row;
    /// args[0] is the source and args[1] the projection, as in streaming sum.
    /// The existing column extremum encoding is unchanged.
    ExtremumOf { max: bool, domain: bool },
    Divmod { remainder_sink: String },
    /// Checked exact integer division with an author-selected rounding rule.
    // @ref LLP 3000.013.001#2-rounded-division-table — the mode is a closed wire enum and evaluation never uses floating point.
    Divide {
        rounding: Rounding,
        /// Decimal places requested by the four-argument surface. `None`
        /// preserves the dividend's already-scaled representation.
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
        scale: Option<u8>,
    },
    Sort { keys: Vec<SortKey> },
    MentionTokens,
    SearchPresent,
    /// A query row assertion cannot classify a metered-out point as absent.
    RequireUncapped,
    DeterministicShare,
    Count,
    Sum,
    CountCapped,
    /// `connected[p]`: how many connections the principal holds open on the
    /// server now; `exists connected[p]` is `> 0`. A device answers 1.
    Connected,
}

impl Builtin {
    pub fn arity(&self) -> usize {
        match self {
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            Self::JobQuery { .. } => 1,
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            Self::JobAs => 2,
            Self::ReceiptSettled { .. } | Self::Extremum { .. } | Self::ProjectFields { .. } | Self::OptionalField { .. } | Self::Calendar { .. } | Self::Civil(_) | Self::Group { .. } | Self::First | Self::Last | Self::Len | Self::Lower | Self::Trim | Self::Sha256 | Self::Sort { .. } | Self::MentionTokens | Self::SearchPresent | Self::RequireUncapped | Self::Count | Self::Sum | Self::CountCapped | Self::Connected => 1,
            Self::ExtremumOf { .. } | Self::At | Self::Concat | Self::Join | Self::Min | Self::Max | Self::Divmod { .. } | Self::Divide { .. } => 2,
            Self::Slice | Self::DeterministicShare => 3,
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum Rounding {
    TowardZero,
    AwayFromZero,
    Floor,
    Floor,
    Ceiling,
    HalfAwayFromZero,
    HalfEven,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct SortKey {
    pub field: String,
    pub order: Order,
    /// The declared private field can be omitted by row projection.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")]
    pub optional: bool,
}

/// A point read: one indexed key, all components bound.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Read {
    pub site: String,
    pub table: String,
    pub index: String,
    pub key: Vec<Expr>,
    pub provenance: KeyProvenance,
}

/// One index-key component of a scan prefix. Snapback 2 spelled a search
/// atom here too; Snapback 4 keeps only the scalar form.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum IndexKeyExpr {
    Scalar(Expr),
    WordPrefix(Expr),
}

impl IndexKeyExpr {
    pub fn scalar(&self) -> Option<&Expr> {
        match self {
            Self::Scalar(expr) => Some(expr),
            Self::WordPrefix(_) => None,
        }
    }
}

/// Stable presence identity: fixed storage and lossless JSON on every host.
pub type VariantId = [u32; 4];

/// A bounded range over an index prefix.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Scan {
    pub site: String,
    pub table: String,
    pub index: String,
    pub prefix: Vec<IndexKeyExpr>,
    pub order: Order,
    pub take: Bound,
    /// A program existence test consumes at most its first match below page ownership.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
    pub existence: bool,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub after: Option<Box<Expr>>,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub before: Option<Box<Expr>>,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub range: Option<Range>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub filter: Option<Box<Expr>>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
    pub opaque_cursor: bool,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub merge_site: Option<String>,
    pub provenance: KeyProvenance,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub variant: Option<crate::ir::VariantId>,
    /// Leading equalities moved into this prefix; zero keeps strict cursor scope.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_zero")]
    pub cursor_pins: u32,
}

impl Scan {
    /// True only for an ordinary index ordered by this column after its pins.
    /// Kept shared by execution and the cost certificate.
    pub fn orders_field(&self, schema: &crate::schema::Schema, field: &str) -> bool {
        let Some(index) = schema.table(&self.table).and_then(|table| table.index(&self.index)) else { return false; };
        // Expiring maintained totals are refreshed while reading. Their
        // stored index value does not order the live value returned today.
        if field == "value" && schema.maintain_for(&self.table).is_some_and(|maintain|
            schema.table(&maintain.source).is_some_and(|source| source.expire.is_some())) { return false; }
        self.prefix.iter().all(|key| matches!(key, IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(_)))
            && matches!(index.components.get(self.prefix.len()), Some(crate::schema::IndexComponent::Column(column)) if column == field)
    }
}

/// A union owns its projected cursor descriptor, including when no branch survives.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct ViewPosition {
    pub take: u32,
    pub order: Order,
    pub after: Option<Box<Expr>>,
    pub suffix: Vec<crate::cursor::CursorColumn>,
}

/// A merge owns its lane discovery and ordered scans; no materialized union.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Merge {
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub position: Option<ViewPosition>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub view: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
    pub order_keys: Vec<String>,
    pub site: String,
    pub lanes: Vec<MergeLane>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub variant: Option<crate::ir::VariantId>,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct MergeLane {
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub guard: Option<Expr>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub distinct: Option<Expr>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub branch: Option<String>,
    /// Evaluated with $project bound to a rule-visible, filtered source row.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub project: Option<Expr>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
    pub setup: Vec<Stmt>,
    pub scan: Scan,
    pub source: Option<LaneSource>,
    pub binding: String,
}

/// Query lane domains reuse statement-loop binding and evaluation. The scan
/// variant retains its wire shape for the frozen conformance programs.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
#[serde(untagged)]
pub enum LaneSource {
    Scan(Scan),
    Range { start: Expr, end_exclusive: Expr, max: Option<u64> },
    List { value: Expr, max: u32 },

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) fn request(dir: &Path, command: Command) -> Result<Value, String> {
    request_observed(dir,command, |_|{})
}

/// Submission remains in flight after a lost reply, even before history shows a commit.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — source cleanup needs either no submission or a definitive completed reply.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) fn request_observed(dir: &Path, command: Command, mut pending: impl FnMut(bool)) -> Result<Value, String> {
    let store = dir.join("store.sqlite");
    let unavailable = |reason: String| {
        let owner_path = store.canonicalize().unwrap_or_else(|_| store.clone());
        let detail = if reason.contains("No such file or directory") {
            "this deployment has no release control socket".to_owned()
        } else {
            reason
        };
        format!(
            "{}; release control unavailable: {detail}; stop `snapback4 serve {}`, rerun the exact command, then `snapback4 serve {}`",
            crate::store_owner::busy(&owner_path),
            dir.display(),
            dir.display()
        )
    };
    let path = address(dir, false).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    let before = socket_entry(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    let mut stream = UnixStream::connect(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    peer(&stream).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    let after = socket_entry(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    if (before.dev(), before.ino()) != (after.dev(), after.ino()) {
        return Err(unavailable(
            "release control socket changed before send".into(),
        ));
    }
    stream
        .set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(120)))
        .map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    stream
        .set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(5)))
        .map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    let bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&Envelope {
        identity: identity(&store)?,
        command,
    })
    .unwrap();
    if bytes.len() > INPUT {
        return Err("release control command exceeds 4096 bytes".into());
    }
    let ambiguous = |reason: io::Error| {
        format!(
            "release command result is unknown: {reason}; run `snapback4 releases {}` to inspect database authority; the server continues admitted work. Do not automatically deploy another release",
            dir.display()
        )
    };
    pending(true);
    stream.write_all(&bytes).map_err(ambiguous)?;
    stream
        .shutdown(std::net::Shutdown::Write)
        .map_err(ambiguous)?;
    let mut reply = Vec::new();
    stream
        .take((OUTPUT + 1) as u64)
        .read_to_end(&mut reply)
        .map_err(ambiguous)?;
    if reply.len() > OUTPUT {
        return Err("release reply too large; inspect releases before retrying".into());
    }
    let value: Value = serde_json::from_slice(&reply).map_err(|e| {
        format!(
            "release result is unknown ({e}); inspect `snapback4 releases {}` before retrying",
            dir.display()
        )
    })?;
    match (value.get("ok"),value.get("error").and_then(Value::as_str)) {
        (Some(value),_)=>{pending(false);Ok(value.clone())},
        (_,Some(error))=>{pending(false);Err(error.into())},
        _=>Err("release result is unknown (invalid reply); inspect releases before retrying".into()),
    }
}

#[cfg(not(unix))]
pub(crate) fn request(_: &Path, _: Command) -> Result<Value, String> {
    Err("local release control requires Unix".into())
}

                if key.alternate {

                    self.sources.insert(location.clone(), (site.clone(), format!("delete {table}")));
                    out.push(Stmt::DeleteTarget { target: Read { site: location, table: table.clone(), index: key.index, key: key.key, provenance: KeyProvenance::Shape } });
                } else {
                    out.push(Stmt::Delete { table: table.clone(), key: key.key[0].clone() });
                }
                Ok(valued.then(|| (Expr::Literal(Value::Null), Ty::Null)))

        }
    }

    /// `t[row]` where `row` is a present row of `t`: the same read as `t[row.id]`.
    fn row_as_key(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<ReadExpr> {
        if read.keys.len() != 1 || read.keys[0].column.is_some() || read.range.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.index.is_some() || !read.order_keys.is_empty() { return None; }
        let key = &read.keys[0].value;
        if !matches!(key, A::Name { .. }) { return None; }
        let (_, ty) = self.clone().expr(key, &mut setup::Body::default()).ok()?;
        if !matches!(ty, Ty::Row(ref table) | Ty::WriteRow(ref table) if *table == read.table) { return None; }
        let mut by_id = read.clone();
    fn merge(&mut self, merge: &ast::MergeExpr, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if merge.lanes.iter().any(|lane| lane.planned.is_some()) || merge.index.is_empty() { return self.planned_view(merge, out, prefix::Position::Page); }
        let info = self.table(&merge.table, &merge.site)?;
        if merge.lanes.first().and_then(|lane| lane.table.as_deref()).is_some_and(|table| table != merge.table) {
            return Err(type_error(&merge.site, "the first merge lane must use the header table, which owns the cursor", "start with [prefix] for the header table").guide("feeds"));
        }
        let Some(index) = info.indexes.iter().find(|index| index.name == merge.index).cloned() else {
            return Err(unknown(&merge.site, format!("{} has no index {}", merge.table, merge.index), name_rewrite(&merge.index, info.indexes.iter().map(|i| i.name.as_str()), None)));
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — matching merges lower to scoped matching scans in the existing lane IR.
        let direction = if merge.order == ast::Order::Asc { "first" } else { "last" };
        let matching_form = |scope: &[String]| format!("merge {} matching q{} {direction} {} by {}:\n  [{}]", merge.table, if merge.cursor.is_some() { " after c" } else { "" }, merge.take, merge.index, scope.join(", "));
        if let Some((position, _)) = &index.words {
            let scope = &index.columns[..*position];
            if merge.matching.is_none() {
                return Err(unsupported(&merge.site, "merge over a words index without matching", matching_form(scope)).guide("search"));
            }
            if merge.lanes.iter().any(|lane| lane.prefix.len() != *position || lane.columns.as_ref().is_some_and(|columns| columns.iter().flatten().any(|column| !scope.contains(column)))) {
                return Err(type_error(&merge.site, format!("matching merge lanes must pin exactly the {} scope column(s) before words in {}.{}", position, merge.table, merge.index), matching_form(scope)).guide("search"));
            }
        } else if merge.matching.is_some() {
            return Err(unsupported(&merge.site, "matching on a merge over a plain index", format!("merge {} {direction} {} by {}:\n  [prefix]  -- or merge {} matching q {direction} {} by <wordsIndex>:\n  [scope]", merge.table, merge.take, merge.index, merge.table, merge.take)).guide("search"));
        }
        let width = merge.lanes.first().map(|lane| lane.prefix.len()).unwrap_or(0);
        if width >= index.columns.len() {
            return Err(type_error(&merge.site, format!("merge lane prefixes must leave at least one {}.{} suffix column", merge.table, merge.index), "drop the last prefix key from every lane"));
        }
        if merge.lanes.iter().any(|lane| lane.prefix.len() != width) {
            return Err(type_error(&merge.site, "merge lanes must have equal-width index prefixes", format!("use {width} prefix key(s) in every lane")));
        }
        let mut cursor_expr = None;
        if let Some(cursor) = &merge.cursor {
            let (e, t) = self.expr(cursor, out)?;
            if !matches!(t.required(), Ty::Cursor) {
                return Err(type_error(cursor.site(), format!("merge after expects a cursor, got {}", t.name()), "declare c: cursor ? and pass after c"));
            }
            cursor_expr = Some(e);
        }
        let merge_site = self.site();
        let matching = merge.matching.as_ref().map(|value| self.expr(value, out)).transpose()?;
        let matching = matching.map(|(value, ty)| {
            let name = self.temp("matching");
            let runtime = self.declare(&name, ty);
            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: runtime, value });
            Box::new(A::Name { name, site: merge.site.clone() })
        });
        let mut lanes = Vec::new();
        for lane in &merge.lanes {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.3 — inbox and public broadcast projections share a row and ordering, not an audience.
            let table = lane.table.as_deref().unwrap_or(&merge.table);
            if table != merge.table {
                let other = self.table(table, &lane.site)?;
                let other_index = other.indexes.iter().find(|i| i.name == merge.index);
                if info.columns != other.columns || other_index.is_none_or(|i| i.columns != index.columns || i.unique != index.unique || i.words != index.words || self.schema.tables[table].indexes[&i.name] != self.schema.tables[&merge.table].indexes[&index.name]) || merge.matching.is_some() {
                    return Err(type_error(&lane.site, "a named-table merge lane needs the same columns and plain index as its header table", format!("give {table} the columns and {} index of {}, or read it separately", merge.index, merge.table)).guide("feeds"));
                }
            }
            self.push_scope();
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let mut source = None;
            let mut binding = String::new();
            if let Some((name, domain)) = &lane.loop_ {
                // Source keys, seeks and predicate setup precede discovery; only
                // the lane prefix may depend on the source row.
                let (domain, ty) = self.lane_domain(domain, out)?;
                source = Some(domain);
                binding = self.declare(name, ty);
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — filtered discovery lanes keep their selector in eager setup; lazy discovery freezes only prefixes.
                if merge.filter.as_ref().is_some_and(|filter| source.as_ref().is_some_and(|source| source.scan().is_some()) || lanes::filter_uses_lane(filter, name)) {
                    let source_binding = self.temp("source");
                    setup.push(Stmt::Let { name: binding, value: ir::var(&source_binding) });
                    binding = source_binding;
                }
            }
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — a merge predicate uses each lane's existing metered filter, before a candidate can be emitted.
            let read = ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: table.to_owned(), keys: lane.prefix.iter().enumerate().map(|(position, value)| ast::KeyPart { column: lane.columns.as_ref().and_then(|c| c.get(position).cloned().flatten()), value: value.clone(), site: value.site().clone() }).collect(), index: Some(merge.index.clone()), range: Some(ast::RangeSpec { order: merge.order, take: merge.take, cursor: None, site: lane.site.clone() }), matching: matching.clone(), filter: merge.filter.clone(), seeks: Vec::new(), site: lane.site.clone() };
            let (mut scan, _) = self.scan(&read, &mut setup)?;
            self.pop_scope();
            scan.after = cursor_expr.clone().map(Box::new);
            scan.opaque_cursor = cursor_expr.is_some();
            scan.merge_site = Some(merge_site.clone());
            if let Some((_, domain)) = &lane.loop_ {
                self.sources.insert(format!("for/{binding}"), (domain.site().clone(), self.catalog.source.expression(domain)));
            }
            lanes.push(MergeLane { guard: None, distinct: None, branch: None, project: None, setup: setup.code, scan, source, binding });
        }
        Ok((Expr::Merge(Merge { variant: self.variant(), position: None, view: None, order_keys: Vec::new(), site: merge_site, lanes }), Ty::Page(merge.table.clone(), merge.take)))
    }

use snapback4_lang::{compile,client_contracts::compare};
fn main() {
 let table=\"table items:\\n  n: int 0..10\\n  label: text 1..20\\n  parent: items ?\\n  public\\n  update <- allow\\n\";

  \"query q():\\n  page = items first 10 by .id\\n  return min(page, .n)\\n\",

  let new=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),format!("{table}{body}").replace("int 0..10","int 0..20"))]);
  println!("CASE {body}diagnostics {:?} / {:?}",old.diagnostics,new.diagnostics);
  if old.diagnostics.is_empty() && new.diagnostics.is_empty() {

  }
 }
}
'''

use snapback4_lang::{compile,client_contracts::compare};
fn main(){
 let table=\"table items:\\n  n: int 0..10\\n  label: text 1..20\\n  unused: int 0..10\\n  public\\n  update <- allow\\n\";
 let body=\"mutation q(id: items):\\n  row = update items[id] {n: 1}\\n  return {label: row.label}\\n\";
 let source=format"'!("{table}{body}");let old=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.clone())]);
 for (label,source) in [("returned text",source.replace("text 1..20","text 0..20")),("unused column",source.replace("unused: int 0..10","unused: int 0..20"))]{
  let new=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source)]);println!("{label}: diagnostics {:?} / {:?}; reports {:?}",old.diagnostics,new.diagnostics,compare(&old,&new,"release 1"));
 }
}
'"'''
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='rel5-review-') as d:
 src=pathlib.Path(d)/'probe.rs';src.write_text(code);binary=pathlib.Path(d)/'probe'
 lib=max(deps.glob('libsnapback4_lang-*.rlib'),key=lambda p:p.stat().st_mtime)

 r=subprocess.run(cmd,capture_output=True,text=True,timeout=35);print(r.stdout,r.stderr)
 if r.returncode==0:
  r=subprocess.run([str(binary)],capture_output=True,text=True,timeout=10);print(r.stdout,r.stderr)

I reproduced a remaining gap: projecting a field from `update` loses its source-domain evidence, so changing a returned integer range or text minimum produces an empty compatibility report. Aggregate extrema and projected `walk` results also miss that evidence. The private probes compiled cleanly; the unused-column control stayed quiet.

## Older spellings that still compile

Existing explicit indexes, merges, fixed chunks, aggregates and timelines keep
their meaning. New code uses predicates, views and `for` sweeps. Following's
view declaration is shown above; `guide feeds` owns its complete example.

```quarry
table oldEvents:
  owner: principal
  at: time
  by byOwner: owner, at, id
  public
  online only
maintain oldCounts: count oldEvents by owner
  read <- allow
query oldPage(c: cursor ?):
  return oldEvents[viewer] last 20 by byOwner after c
query oldMerged(c: cursor ?):
  return merge oldEvents after c last 20 by byOwner:
    [viewer]
job oldSweep():
  chunk e in oldEvents first 10 by byId:
    delete oldEvents[e.id]
-- For Following, declare `maintain timeline: timeline`; `guide feeds` has the complete app.
table timelineProfiles:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  cap 1008
  immutable principal
  public 'timelineProfiles'
  insert <- .principal = viewer

--- a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md

@@ -1,5 +1,9 @@
 # Snapback 4 behavior changes
 
+## 0.4.34
+
+- Guide changes: `guide deploy` explains retained-source reactivation with the same release id, atomic refused-candidate cleanup, the shared `--restore-dormant` intent and deploy-time rollback foresight with bridge commands. Old-client compatibility reports compare operation inputs, results and refusals; required new inputs remain required in generated types. The executable v1/v2 recipe uses literal query/mutation defaults, ignored deprecated inputs and same-module operation aliases. Availability accepts extra optional served arguments. Migration recipes and staging clock changes are owned by the data lifecycle workstream; security invariants/audit by the security workstream.
+
 ## 0.4.33
 
 - Declare independent security invariants beside access rules. `check` reports proved, refuted with a replayable counterexample, or unproved. Supported local rule implications, typed aggregate contributor audiences and exact server lifetime admission have scoped proofs; general temporal histories, opaque writers, capture/live-parent reductions and disconnected-device freshness remain explicitly unproved. Passing finite challenges is not a universal security proof.

codex
One material concern remains at `03541d222c63001c5e5434dd836fe86b4af5368f`.

**[P2] Result-domain tracing still silently misses existing returned-value paths.** In [result_domains.rs:11](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-rel5/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/result_domains.rs:11), definitions omit write-result bindings; line 17 handles only Page/DerivedArray loops; line 34 recognizes field selection only through `Field`.

I reproduced three cleanly compiling cases where changing `items.n` from `int 0..10` to `int 0..20` leaves `client_contracts::compare` completely empty:

- `row = update items[id] {label: 'x'}` followed by `return {n: row.n}`. The returned variable originates in `WriteTarget.result`, which has no definition edge.
- `chain = walk items[id] up .parent depth 8` followed by `return chain {n, label}`. The `For.binding` originates in `LoopDomain::Recurse`, which is ignored.
- `page = items first 10 by .id` followed by `return min(page, .n)`. The selected column is encoded in `Builtin::Extremum.field`, which the tracer ignores.

A mutation returning `row.label` likewise produces no report when its declared text domain changes from `text 1..20` to `text 0..20`. The unused-column control correctly stays quiet.

These are existing language forms within §11’s returned-value domain comparison. Their TypeScript types remain unchanged, so the fallback comparison at [client_contracts.rs:83](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-rel5/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/client_contracts.rs:83) cannot recover the missing warning.

**Repair:** connect write-result bindings to their target-table provenance, propagate Recurse bindings, and collect column selectors carried by aggregate builtins. Add focused changed-domain and unused-column controls for these paths.

The ambiguous-submission concern appears addressed: pending state is established before socket writes, survives lost replies, and prevents deletion while a later commit remains possible. All seven focused language tests and four candidate-related tests passed, including `candidate_survives_lost_reply_then_later_commit_and_restart`. Broader qualification was not run. No repository files were edited.
